```
                401,
                request_id,
                secure_transport=secure,
            )

            await response(scope, receive, send_secure)

        except AccessDenied:
            with self._lock:
                self._rejected += 1

            response = make_security_error_response(
                "ACCESS_DENIED",
                403,
                request_id,
                secure_transport=secure,
            )

            await response(scope, receive, send_secure)

        except ConfigurationFailure:
            with self._lock:
                self._rejected += 1

            response = make_security_error_response(
                "CONFIG_ERROR",
                503,
                request_id,
                secure_transport=secure,
            )

            await response(scope, receive, send_secure)


# ============================================================
# 10 — REGISTRATION HELPER
# ============================================================

def register_security_middleware(
    app: FastAPI,
    settings: AppSettings | None = None,
) -> None:
    """
    Register middleware before serving HTTP requests.

    FastAPI middleware registration must happen during
    application construction, before application startup.
    """

    active = settings if settings is not None else get_settings()

    app.add_middleware(
        SinusSecurityMiddleware,
        settings=active,
    )


# ============================================================
# END OF CENTRAL HTTP SECURITY MIDDLEWARE
# ============================================================
```

### sinus-viewer/backend/app/security/path_guard.py
```
"""
SINUS VIEWER — READ-ONLY DATASET PATH GUARD
===========================================

Centralized protection for original DICOM dataset access.

Features:
- Restrict paths to a configured dataset root.
- Reject traversal components and symbolic links.
- Open files using POSIX directory file descriptors.
- Read-only binary file access with O_NOFOLLOW.
- Reject devices, sockets, FIFOs and nonregular files.
- Keep runtime storage separate from source dataset.
- Provide controlled errors without sensitive paths.
- No source file creation, writing or deletion.

Deployment target:
    Google Colab / Linux with POSIX dir_fd support.

This module deliberately fails closed on platforms that
lack the required secure opening primitives.

IMPORTANT:
- Importing this module performs no filesystem operations.
- Security checks are not a replacement for authentication.
- All HTTP requests must be authorized before path access.
- Source files must never be opened with write flags.
- Existing scanner/decoder integrations must be migrated
  to this guard in the integration phase.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import stat
from contextlib import contextmanager
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path
from typing import BinaryIO, Iterator

from ..core.config import AppSettings, get_settings


# ============================================================
# 01 — CONTROLLED ERRORS
# ============================================================

class PathGuardError(RuntimeError):
    """Base path-security error without filesystem details."""

    public_code = "PATH_REJECTED"

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        super().__init__(self.public_code)


class PathAccessDenied(PathGuardError):
    """Path outside the permitted dataset or unsafe component."""


class PathNotFound(PathGuardError):
    """Requested source is missing or inaccessible."""


class PathTypeRejected(PathGuardError):
    """Expected file/directory type does not match."""


class PathPlatformUnsupported(PathGuardError):
    """Required secure filesystem primitives unavailable."""


# ============================================================
# 02 — IMMUTABLE SOURCE DESCRIPTION
# ============================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GuardedFileInfo:
    size_bytes: int
    device_id: int
    inode: int

    def safe_summary(self) -> dict:
        # Device/inode identifiers deliberately not exposed.
        return {
            "size_bytes": self.size_bytes,
            "regular_file": True,
        }


# ============================================================
# 03 — PLATFORM SECURITY CHECK
# ============================================================

def require_secure_posix() -> None:
    """
    Require descriptor-relative Linux/POSIX file operations.

    No silent downgrade to ordinary pathname-based opening.
    """

    if os.name != "posix":
        raise PathPlatformUnsupported()

    required_flags = (
        "O_RDONLY",
        "O_DIRECTORY",
        "O_NOFOLLOW",
        "O_CLOEXEC",
    )

    if any(not hasattr(os, name) for name in required_flags):
        raise PathPlatformUnsupported()

    if (
        os.open not in os.supports_dir_fd
        or os.stat not in os.supports_dir_fd
    ):
        raise PathPlatformUnsupported()


# ============================================================
# 04 — PATH COMPONENT VALIDATION
# ============================================================

def _split_components(value: str) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    """
    Split POSIX path components without trusting normalization.

    Explicitly rejects parent traversal, null bytes and
    alternate path separator syntax.
    """

    if not isinstance(value, str) or not value:
        raise PathAccessDenied()

    if "\x00" in value or "\\" in value:
        raise PathAccessDenied()

    components = tuple(
        component
        for component in value.split("/")
        if component not in ("", ".")
    )

    if any(component == ".." for component in components):
        raise PathAccessDenied()

    return components


def _absolute_components(path: str | Path) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    raw = os.fspath(path)

    if not isinstance(raw, str) or not raw.startswith("/"):
        raise PathAccessDenied()

    return _split_components(raw)


# ============================================================
# 05 — CONFIGURED ROOT POLICY
# ============================================================

def get_dataset_root(
    settings: AppSettings | None = None,
) -> Path:
    """
    Return configured dataset root without resolving links.

    Symlinks are checked during descriptor-based opening.
    """

    active = settings if settings is not None else get_settings()

    root = active.storage.dataset.root_path

    if not root.is_absolute():
        raise PathAccessDenied()

    _absolute_components(root)

    return root


def check_runtime_isolation(
    settings: AppSettings | None = None,
) -> None:
    """
    Reject overlapping dataset and runtime storage paths.

    This compares lexical components. Both trees must also
    be validated independently at their actual use sites.
    """

    active = settings if settings is not None else get_settings()

    dataset = _absolute_components(
        active.storage.dataset.root_path
    )

    runtime = _absolute_components(
        active.storage.runtime.root_path
    )

    if not dataset or not runtime:
        raise PathAccessDenied()

    def prefix(a: tuple[str, ...], b: tuple[str, ...]) -> bool:
        return len(a) <= len(b) and b[:len(a)] == a

    if prefix(dataset, runtime) or prefix(runtime, dataset):
        raise PathAccessDenied()


# ============================================================
# 06 — RESOLVE ALLOWED RELATIVE COMPONENTS
# ============================================================

def allowed_relative_parts(
    path: str | Path,
    settings: AppSettings | None = None,
    *,
    allow_root: bool = False,
) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    """
    Validate lexical containment inside dataset root.

    Relative paths are interpreted relative to that root.
    Absolute paths must start with the exact root components.

    This does not access the filesystem.
    """

    root_parts = _absolute_components(
        get_dataset_root(settings)
    )

    raw = os.fspath(path)

    if not isinstance(raw, str) or not raw:
        raise PathAccessDenied()

    if raw.startswith("/"):
        parts = _absolute_components(raw)

        if parts[:len(root_parts)] != root_parts:
            raise PathAccessDenied()

        relative = parts[len(root_parts):]
    else:
        relative = _split_components(raw)

    if not relative and not allow_root:
        raise PathAccessDenied()

    return relative


# ============================================================
# 07 — DESCRIPTOR-RELATIVE DIRECTORY ACCESS
# ============================================================

@contextmanager
def _open_directory_chain(
    absolute_parts: tuple[str, ...],
) -> Iterator[int]:
    """
    Safely traverse directories from filesystem root.

    Every path component is opened with O_NOFOLLOW and
    O_DIRECTORY; symlinked components are rejected.

    Uses no writable file descriptors.
    """

    require_secure_posix()

    flags = (
        os.O_RDONLY
        | os.O_DIRECTORY
        | os.O_NOFOLLOW
        | os.O_CLOEXEC
    )

    fd: int | None = None

    try:
        fd = os.open("/", flags)

        for part in absolute_parts:
            next_fd = os.open(
                part,
                flags,
                dir_fd=fd,
            )

            os.close(fd)
            fd = next_fd

        yield fd

    except (OSError, ValueError):
        raise PathAccessDenied() from None

    finally:
        if fd is not None:
            os.close(fd)


@contextmanager
def open_guarded_directory(
    path: str | Path,
    settings: AppSettings | None = None,
) -> Iterator[int]:
    """
    Open a directory beneath the authorized root.

    Caller receives a read-only directory descriptor.
    The descriptor is closed when the context ends.

    Do not expose this descriptor or its path to browser APIs.
    """

    active = settings if settings is not None else get_settings()

    check_runtime_isolation(active)

    relative = allowed_relative_parts(
        path,
        active,
        allow_root=True,
    )

    components = (
        _absolute_components(get_dataset_root(active))
        + relative
    )

    with _open_directory_chain(components) as fd:
        yield fd


# ============================================================
# 08 — SECURE READ-ONLY FILE OPENING
# ============================================================

@contextmanager
def open_guarded_binary(
    path: str | Path,
    settings: AppSettings | None = None,
    *,
    max_bytes: int | None = None,
) -> Iterator[tuple[BinaryIO, GuardedFileInfo]]:
    """
    Open a regular dataset file with read-only flags.

    Security:
    - Traversal rejected before opening.
    - Every directory component checked with O_NOFOLLOW.
    - Final file opened with O_NOFOLLOW.
    - Final descriptor verified using fstat().
    - No write/create/truncate flags used.

    Unlike returning a validated pathname, the opened
    descriptor remains tied to the verified file object.

    Intended pattern:
        with open_guarded_binary(path, settings) as (stream, info):
            dataset = pydicom.dcmread(
                stream,
                stop_before_pixels=True,
            )
    """

    active = settings if settings is not None else get_settings()

    check_runtime_isolation(active)

    parts = allowed_relative_parts(path, active)

    if not parts:
        raise PathAccessDenied()

    if max_bytes is not None and (
        not isinstance(max_bytes, int)
        or isinstance(max_bytes, bool)
        or max_bytes < 1
    ):
        raise ValueError("Invalid maximum file size.")

    parent_parts = (
        _absolute_components(get_dataset_root(active))
        + parts[:-1]
    )

    filename = parts[-1]

    flags = (
        os.O_RDONLY
        | os.O_NOFOLLOW
        | os.O_CLOEXEC
        | getattr(os, "O_NONBLOCK", 0)
    )

    with _open_directory_chain(parent_parts) as parent_fd:
        fd: int | None = None

        try:
            fd = os.open(
                filename,
                flags,
                dir_fd=parent_fd,
            )

            details = os.fstat(fd)

            if not stat.S_ISREG(details.st_mode):
                raise PathTypeRejected()

            if (
                max_bytes is not None
                and details.st_size > max_bytes
            ):
                raise PathTypeRejected()

            info = GuardedFileInfo(
                size_bytes=int(details.st_size),
                device_id=int(details.st_dev),
                inode=int(details.st_ino),
            )

            stream = os.fdopen(fd, "rb", closefd=True)
            fd = None

            with stream:
                yield stream, info

        except PathGuardError:
            raise

        except (OSError, ValueError):
            raise PathNotFound() from None

        finally:
            if fd is not None:
                os.close(fd)


# ============================================================
# 09 — READ-ONLY FILE INFORMATION
# ============================================================

def inspect_guarded_file(
    path: str | Path,
    settings: AppSettings | None = None,
    *,
    max_bytes: int | None = None,
) -> GuardedFileInfo:
    """
    Inspect a regular file through a secure descriptor.

    Does not read pixel data or modify original content.
    """

    with open_guarded_binary(
        path,
        settings,
        max_bytes=max_bytes,
    ) as (_, info):
        return info


# ============================================================
# 10 — PATH GUARD SERVICE WRAPPER
# ============================================================

class DatasetPathGuard:
    """
    Small reusable interface for DICOM reader modules.

    Actual dataset access remains read-only and occurs
    only inside explicit method calls.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        settings: AppSettings | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self._settings = (
            settings if settings is not None
            else get_settings()
        )

        check_runtime_isolation(self._settings)

    def validate_relative(
        self,
        path: str | Path,
        *,
        allow_root: bool = False,
    ) -> tuple[str, ...]:
        return allowed_relative_parts(
            path,
            self._settings,
            allow_root=allow_root,
        )

    def inspect_file(
        self,
        path: str | Path,
        *,
        max_bytes: int | None = None,
    ) -> GuardedFileInfo:
        return inspect_guarded_file(
            path,
            self._settings,
            max_bytes=max_bytes,
        )

    @contextmanager
    def open_file(
        self,
        path: str | Path,
        *,
        max_bytes: int | None = None,
    ) -> Iterator[tuple[BinaryIO, GuardedFileInfo]]:
        with open_guarded_binary(
            path,
            self._settings,
            max_bytes=max_bytes,
        ) as result:
            yield result

    @contextmanager
    def open_directory(
        self,
        path: str | Path,
    ) -> Iterator[int]:
        with open_guarded_directory(
            path,
            self._settings,
        ) as fd:
            yield fd


# ============================================================
# 11 — FACTORY
# ============================================================

def create_dataset_path_guard(
    settings: AppSettings | None = None,
) -> DatasetPathGuard:
    """
    Create a guard without reading patient data.

    Runtime capability checks occur during guarded
    filesystem operations.
    """

    return DatasetPathGuard(settings)


# ============================================================
# END OF DATASET PATH GUARD
# ============================================================
```

### sinus-viewer/backend/app/security/session.py
```
"""
SINUS VIEWER — FASTAPI SESSION SECURITY
=======================================

Provides:
- AuthenticationService retrieval from FastAPI app state.
- HttpOnly cookie-based session authentication.
- Trusted AuthIdentity injection through FastAPI Depends.
- Server-side owner identity for dataset/volume services.
- Cross-origin protection for unsafe HTTP methods.
- Fetch Metadata protection for cross-site requests.
- Explicit configured public origin for origin checks.
- Safe request-state identity access.
- Separate dependency for unauthenticated login routes.

Security boundaries:
- No client-supplied owner ID is accepted as identity.
- Raw tokens, cookies, URLs and request bodies are not logged.
- CSRF protection must be applied to login/logout and all
  cookie-authenticated state-changing API routes.
- Authenticated read endpoints must still validate ownership.
- Reverse-proxy and HTTPS controls are configured elsewhere.
- This module does not grant access merely because a cookie
  is present; the server-side session must be valid.
- Real patient data must not be exposed through an
  unapproved public Colab tunnel.

No dataset access or filesystem writing occurs here.

Python 3.10+
FastAPI / Starlette
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hmac
import os
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Annotated
from urllib.parse import urlsplit

from fastapi import Depends, Request

from ..core.exceptions import (
    AccessDenied,
    AuthenticationRequired,
    ConfigurationFailure,
    SessionExpired,
)
from .auth import (
    AuthIdentity,
    AuthenticationService,
    AuthenticationRequired as AuthSessionRequired,
    AuthenticationServiceClosed,
    SessionExpired as AuthSessionExpired,
    get_session_cookie_name,
)


# ============================================================
# 01 — CONSTANTS
# ============================================================

AUTH_SERVICE_STATE_NAME = "auth_service"

PUBLIC_ORIGIN_ENV = "SINUS_VIEWER_PUBLIC_ORIGIN"

SAFE_HTTP_METHODS = frozenset({
    "GET",
    "HEAD",
    "OPTIONS",
})

UNSAFE_HTTP_METHODS = frozenset({
    "POST",
    "PUT",
    "PATCH",
    "DELETE",
})

ALLOWED_FETCH_SITE = frozenset({
    "same-origin",
    "none",
})

MAX_ORIGIN_LENGTH = 512
MAX_COOKIE_LENGTH = 256

ORIGIN_PATTERN = re.compile(
    r"^https?://[^/?#]+$",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)


# ============================================================
# 02 — TRUSTED CONTEXT
# ============================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AuthenticatedContext:
    """
    Trusted identity available to protected API routes.

    owner_id is produced by AuthenticationService after
    validating the server-side session.
    """

    owner_id: str
    issued_at: float
    expires_at: float

    @classmethod
    def from_identity(
        cls,
        identity: AuthIdentity,
    ) -> "AuthenticatedContext":
        return cls(
            owner_id=identity.owner_id,
            issued_at=identity.issued_at,
            expires_at=identity.expires_at,
        )


# ============================================================
# 03 — AUTHENTICATION SERVICE LOOKUP
# ============================================================

def get_authentication_service(
    request: Request,
) -> AuthenticationService:
    """
    Retrieve service initialized by the FastAPI lifespan.

    Expected startup setup:
        app.state.auth_service = create_authentication_service()

    Never instantiate AuthenticationService per request.
    """

    service = getattr(
        request.app.state,
        AUTH_SERVICE_STATE_NAME,
        None,
    )

    if not isinstance(service, AuthenticationService):
        raise ConfigurationFailure()

    return service


# ============================================================
# 04 — COOKIE EXTRACTION
# ============================================================

def extract_session_token(
    request: Request,
) -> str | None:
    """
    Read bearer token only from the configured cookie.

    Authorization headers, query strings and request bodies
    are deliberately not accepted as alternative sources.
    """

    cookie_name = get_session_cookie_name()

    # Reject duplicate cookie names, which might otherwise
    # be interpreted inconsistently by different components.
    raw_headers = request.headers.getlist("cookie")

    matches: list[str] = []

    for raw_header in raw_headers:
        if len(raw_header) > 8192:
            raise AuthenticationRequired()

        for fragment in raw_header.split(";"):
            name, separator, value = fragment.strip().partition("=")

            if separator and name == cookie_name:
                matches.append(value.strip())

    if not matches:
        return None

    if len(matches) != 1:
        raise AuthenticationRequired()

    token = matches[0]

    if not token or len(token) > MAX_COOKIE_LENGTH:
        raise AuthenticationRequired()

    return token


# ============================================================
# 05 — PUBLIC ORIGIN VALIDATION
# ============================================================

def _normalize_origin(
    origin: str,
) -> str:
    """
    Validate the syntax of one HTTP origin.

    No paths, credentials, fragments or query parameters.
    Public deployment requires HTTPS.
    """

    if (
        not isinstance(origin, str)
        or len(origin) > MAX_ORIGIN_LENGTH
        or not ORIGIN_PATTERN.fullmatch(origin)
    ):
        raise ConfigurationFailure()

    parsed = urlsplit(origin)

    if (
        parsed.scheme not in {"http", "https"}
        or not parsed.hostname
        or parsed.username is not None
        or parsed.password is not None
        or parsed.path
        or parsed.query
        or parsed.fragment
    ):
        raise ConfigurationFailure()

    try:
        port = parsed.port
    except ValueError:
        raise ConfigurationFailure() from None

    if (
        parsed.scheme != "https"
        and parsed.hostname not in {
            "localhost",
            "127.0.0.1",
            "::1",
        }
    ):
        raise ConfigurationFailure()

    host = parsed.hostname.lower()

    if ":" in host:
        host = f"[{host}]"

    default_port = (
        443 if parsed.scheme == "https" else 80
    )

    suffix = (
        f":{port}"
        if port is not None and port != default_port
        else ""
    )

    return f"{parsed.scheme}://{host}{suffix}"


def get_trusted_public_origin() -> str:
    """
    Read explicitly configured browser origin.

    This must be the trusted HTTPS tunnel URL in Colab.
    Never derive it from untrusted Host/X-Forwarded headers.

    Missing configuration fails closed.
    """

    value = os.environ.get(PUBLIC_ORIGIN_ENV)

    if not value:
        raise ConfigurationFailure()

    return _normalize_origin(value)


# ============================================================
# 06 — CROSS-ORIGIN REQUEST PROTECTION
# ============================================================

def validate_request_origin(
    request: Request,
) -> None:
    """
    Enforce Origin and Fetch Metadata checks.

    This is intended for browser cookie-authenticated APIs,
    including login/logout and all unsafe methods.

    For unsafe methods, a valid Origin header is mandatory.
    Cross-site fetches are rejected even for safe methods.

    This does not replace HTTPS, auth or proxy protections.
    """

    method = request.method.upper()

    fetch_sites = request.headers.getlist(
        "sec-fetch-site"
    )

    if len(fetch_sites) > 1:
        raise AccessDenied()

    if fetch_sites:
        fetch_site = fetch_sites[0].strip().lower()

        if fetch_site not in ALLOWED_FETCH_SITE:
            raise AccessDenied()

    if method in SAFE_HTTP_METHODS:
        return

    if method not in UNSAFE_HTTP_METHODS:
        raise AccessDenied()

    origin_headers = request.headers.getlist("origin")

    if len(origin_headers) != 1:
        raise AccessDenied()

    received = origin_headers[0]

    # Validation of malformed request origin must return a
    # client access error, not disclose server configuration.
    try:
        normalized_received = _normalize_origin(received)
    except ConfigurationFailure:
        raise AccessDenied() from None

    trusted = get_trusted_public_origin()

    if not hmac.compare_digest(
        normalized_received,
        trusted,
    ):
        raise AccessDenied()


# ============================================================
# 07 — AUTHENTICATE CURRENT REQUEST
# ============================================================

def authenticate_request(
    request: Request,
    service: AuthenticationService | None = None,
) -> AuthenticatedContext:
    """
    Verify cookie against the server-side session registry.

    Successful validation stores trusted identity on
    request.state for use within this request only.
    """

    auth_service = (
        service
        if service is not None
        else get_authentication_service(request)
    )

    token = extract_session_token(request)

    if token is None:
        raise AuthenticationRequired()

    try:
        identity = auth_service.authenticate(token)

    except AuthSessionExpired:
        raise SessionExpired() from None

    except AuthSessionRequired:
        raise AuthenticationRequired() from None

    except AuthenticationServiceClosed:
        raise ConfigurationFailure() from None

    context = AuthenticatedContext.from_identity(
        identity
    )

    request.state.authenticated_context = context

    return context


# ============================================================
# 08 — FASTAPI AUTHENTICATION DEPENDENCIES
# ============================================================

def require_authenticated_context(
    request: Request,
) -> AuthenticatedContext:
    """
    Apply Origin/Fetch Metadata checks, then authenticate.

    Typical protected route:
        @router.get("/patients")
        def patients(
            auth: Annotated[
                AuthenticatedContext,
                Depends(require_authenticated_context),
            ],
        ):
            return dataset_service.list_patients(
                session_id,
                owner_id=auth.owner_id,
            )
    """

    validate_request_origin(request)

    return authenticate_request(request)


def require_authenticated_owner(
    context: Annotated[
        AuthenticatedContext,
        Depends(require_authenticated_context),
    ],
) -> str:
    """
    Inject only trusted server-side owner identity.

    Never obtain owner_id from request body, query string
    or browser local storage.
    """

    return context.owner_id


def require_safe_login_request(
    request: Request,
) -> None:
    """
    Apply origin protection to unauthenticated login routes.

    Login endpoints must additionally enforce:
    - POST only
    - Request size limits
    - Brute-force throttling
    - HTTPS
    - Safe responses with no user enumeration
    """

    if request.method.upper() != "POST":
        raise AccessDenied()

    validate_request_origin(request)


def require_safe_logout_request(
    request: Request,
) -> AuthenticatedContext:
    """
    Protect logout with authentication and Origin checks.
    """

    if request.method.upper() != "POST":
        raise AccessDenied()

    return require_authenticated_context(request)


# ============================================================
# 09 — READ TRUSTED REQUEST STATE
# ============================================================

def get_authenticated_request_context(
    request: Request,
) -> AuthenticatedContext:
    """
    Retrieve identity already validated in this request.

    This is for use only after an authentication dependency.
    Never populate this state from untrusted request input.
    """

    context = getattr(
        request.state,
        "authenticated_context",
        None,
    )

    if not isinstance(context, AuthenticatedContext):
        raise AuthenticationRequired()

    return context


# ============================================================
# 10 — OPTIONAL SESSION INFORMATION
# ============================================================

def get_authenticated_session_summary(
    context: AuthenticatedContext,
) -> dict:
    """
    Limited frontend session state.

    No bearer token, source path or patient identifiers.
    """

    return {
        "authenticated": True,
        "expires_at": context.expires_at,
    }


# ============================================================
# 11 — SERVICE REGISTRATION
# ============================================================

def register_authentication_service(
    app: object,
    service: AuthenticationService,
) -> None:
    """
    Register the initialized authentication service.

    Intended for FastAPI application lifespan startup.
    """

    if not isinstance(service, AuthenticationService):
        raise TypeError(
            "AuthenticationService required."
        )

    state = getattr(app, "state", None)

    if state is None:
        raise TypeError(
            "FastAPI application state required."
        )

    existing = getattr(
        state,
        AUTH_SERVICE_STATE_NAME,
        None,
    )

    if existing is not None:
        raise RuntimeError(
            "Authentication service already registered."
        )

    setattr(
        state,
        AUTH_SERVICE_STATE_NAME,
        service,
    )


# ============================================================
# END OF FASTAPI SESSION SECURITY
# ============================================================
```

### sinus-viewer/backend/app/services/__init__.py
```

```

### sinus-viewer/backend/app/services/cache_service.py
```
"""
SINUS VIEWER — MEMORY-AWARE VOLUME CACHE
========================================

Features:
- Thread-safe, in-memory volume caching.
- Least-recently-used (LRU) eviction.
- Time-to-live (TTL) expiration.
- Maximum cached-volume count.
- Conservative available-RAM checks.
- Single-flight loading per cache key.
- Safe invalidation and shutdown.
- No patient identifiers in public statistics.
- No disk writes or source DICOM modification.

Limitations:
- Process-local cache, not shared between workers.
- Successful load does not guarantee sufficient RAM later.
- A caller holding a volume reference prevents its memory
  from being released immediately after eviction.
- Cache keys must be opaque internal series handles.
- Source-change detection belongs to the indexing service.

Python 3.10+
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import math
import threading
import time
from collections import OrderedDict
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Callable

import psutil

from ..core.config import AppSettings, get_settings
from ..dicom.volume_builder import VolumePackage


# ============================================================
# 01 — CONSTANTS
# ============================================================

GIB = 1024 ** 3
MIB = 1024 ** 2

VolumeLoader = Callable[[], VolumePackage]


# ============================================================
# 02 — CONTROLLED CACHE ERRORS
# ============================================================

class CacheServiceError(RuntimeError):
    """Base cache error without sensitive metadata."""

    public_code = "PROCESSING_FAILED"

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        super().__init__(self.public_code)


class CacheMemoryLimit(CacheServiceError):
    public_code = "OUT_OF_MEMORY"


class CacheLoadFailed(CacheServiceError):
    public_code = "PROCESSING_FAILED"


class CacheClosed(CacheServiceError):
    public_code = "SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE"


class CacheInvalidKey(CacheServiceError):
    public_code = "INVALID_REQUEST"


# ============================================================
# 03 — INTERNAL CACHE ENTRY
# ============================================================

@dataclass
class CacheEntry:
    key_digest: str
    package: VolumePackage = field(repr=False)

    size_bytes: int
    created_at: float
    last_access_at: float
    expires_at: float

    hits: int = 0


@dataclass
class _PendingLoad:
    """
    One in-progress load.

    The leader signals waiting callers after either
    successfully inserting the result or failing.
    """

    event: threading.Event = field(
        default_factory=threading.Event,
        repr=False,
    )
    error: CacheServiceError | None = None


# ============================================================
# 04 — CACHE STATISTICS
# ============================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CacheStatistics:
    entries: int
    total_bytes: int
    total_mib: float

    hits: int
    misses: int
    evictions: int
    expirations: int

    pending_loads: int
    maximum_entries: int
    ttl_seconds: int

    enabled: bool
    closed: bool

    def safe_summary(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "entries": self.entries,
            "total_mib": self.total_mib,
            "hits": self.hits,
            "misses": self.misses,
            "evictions": self.evictions,
            "expirations": self.expirations,
            "pending_loads": self.pending_loads,
            "maximum_entries": self.maximum_entries,
            "ttl_seconds": self.ttl_seconds,
            "enabled": self.enabled,
            "closed": self.closed,
        }


# ============================================================
# 05 — CACHE KEY HANDLING
# ============================================================

def normalize_cache_key(key: str) -> str:
    """
    Convert an internal identifier into a fixed-length key.

    Hashing prevents accidental exposure in cache inspection.
    It is not anonymization or access authorization.
    """

    if not isinstance(key, str) or not key:
        raise CacheInvalidKey()

    if len(key) > 512:
        raise CacheInvalidKey()

    return hashlib.sha256(
        key.encode("utf-8")
    ).hexdigest()


# ============================================================
# 06 — PACKAGE MEMORY ESTIMATION
# ============================================================

def calculate_package_bytes(
    package: VolumePackage,
) -> int:
    """
    Count memory represented by full and preview arrays.

    Identical NumPy data buffers are counted once.

    This excludes Python object overhead, decoder working
    buffers and references held outside the cache.
    """

    if not isinstance(package, VolumePackage):
        raise CacheLoadFailed()

    total = 0
    seen_buffers: set[int] = set()

    for volume in package.all_levels():
        data = volume.voxels

        pointer = int(
            data.__array_interface__["data"][0]
        )

        if pointer not in seen_buffers:
            seen_buffers.add(pointer)
            total += int(data.nbytes)

    return total


# ============================================================
# 07 — CACHE SERVICE
# ============================================================

class VolumeCacheService:
    """
    Thread-safe cache for prepared medical imaging volumes.

    Use one instance per backend process.

    All public methods protect shared cache state with
    a reentrant lock. User-provided loaders run outside
    the global cache lock.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        settings: AppSettings | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self._settings = (
            settings if settings is not None
            else get_settings()
        )

        config = self._settings.performance.cache

        self._enabled = bool(config.enabled)

        self._maximum_entries = max(
            1,
            int(config.max_in_memory_volumes),
        )

        self._ttl_seconds = max(
            1,
            int(config.cache_ttl_minutes * 60),
        )

        self._entries: OrderedDict[
            str, CacheEntry
        ] = OrderedDict()

        self._pending: dict[
            str, _PendingLoad
        ] = {}

        self._lock = threading.RLock()

        self._hits = 0
        self._misses = 0
        self._evictions = 0
        self._expirations = 0

        self._total_bytes = 0
        self._closed = False

        # A changing generation prevents a load started
        # before clear()/invalidate() from being inserted
        # after that operation completes.
        self._generation = 0

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # INTERNAL STATE
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def _require_open(self) -> None:
        if self._closed:
            raise CacheClosed()

    def _remove_entry(
        self,
        key: str,
        *,
        expired: bool = False,
    ) -> bool:
        entry = self._entries.pop(key, None)

        if entry is None:
            return False

        self._total_bytes = max(
            0,
            self._total_bytes - entry.size_bytes,
        )

        if expired:
            self._expirations += 1
        else:
            self._evictions += 1

        return True

    def _remove_expired(
        self,
        now: float,
    ) -> None:
        expired_keys = [
            key
            for key, entry in self._entries.items()
            if entry.expires_at <= now
        ]

        for key in expired_keys:
            self._remove_entry(
                key,
                expired=True,
            )

    def _lookup(
        self,
        digest: str,
    ) -> VolumePackage | None:
        now = time.monotonic()

        self._remove_expired(now)

        entry = self._entries.get(digest)

        if entry is None:
            return None

        entry.last_access_at = now
        entry.hits += 1

        self._entries.move_to_end(digest)
        self._hits += 1

        return entry.package

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # MEMORY SAFETY
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def _memory_budget_bytes(self) -> int:
        """
        Conservative budget for additional cache memory.

        This is a snapshot, not a reservation.
        """

        memory_config = self._settings.performance.memory

        if not memory_config.enable_memory_guard:
            return 2 ** 63 - 1

        memory = psutil.virtual_memory()

        reserved = int(
            max(
                memory_config.minimum_free_ram_gb,
                self._settings.hardware.cpu.reserve_system_memory_gb,
            ) * GIB
        )

        maximum_used = int(
            memory.total
            * memory_config.max_ram_utilization_percent
            / 100.0
        )

        currently_used = max(
            0,
            memory.total - memory.available,
        )

        return max(
            0,
            min(
                memory.available - reserved,
                maximum_used - currently_used,
            ),
        )

    def _check_package_size(
        self,
        package_size: int,
    ) -> None:
        """
        Avoid accepting oversized cache objects.

        The volume has already been built when this method
        runs. The check therefore cannot prevent memory
        consumption during the initial decode.
        """

        if package_size <= 0:
            raise CacheLoadFailed()

        maximum_raw = (
            self._settings.dicom.volume
            .max_uncompressed_volume_mb
            * MIB
        )

        maximum_cache = (
            self._settings.performance.cache
            .max_disk_cache_gb
            * GIB
        )

        if package_size > maximum_raw:
            raise CacheMemoryLimit()

        # This configured value is primarily a disk-cache
        # limit. It is used here only as an additional
        # conservative upper bound, not as a RAM target.
        if package_size > maximum_cache:
            raise CacheMemoryLimit()

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # GET
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def get(
        self,
        key: str,
    ) -> VolumePackage | None:
        digest = normalize_cache_key(key)

        with self._lock:
            self._require_open()

            if not self._enabled:
                return None

            result = self._lookup(digest)

            if result is None:
                self._misses += 1

            return result

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # PUT
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def put(
        self,
        key: str,
        package: VolumePackage,
    ) -> bool:
        """
        Cache one prepared volume package.

        Returns False when caching is disabled.
        """

        digest = normalize_cache_key(key)

        if not self._enabled:
            with self._lock:
                self._require_open()
            return False

        package_size = calculate_package_bytes(package)

        self._check_package_size(package_size)

        with self._lock:
            self._require_open()

            self._remove_expired(time.monotonic())

            # An already-built package is resident in RAM.
            # Inserting its reference does not normally
            # allocate a second pixel buffer.
            self._entries.pop(digest, None)

            # Recalculate accounting after replacement.
            self._total_bytes = sum(
                entry.size_bytes
                for entry in self._entries.values()
            )

            while len(self._entries) >= self._maximum_entries:
                oldest_key = next(iter(self._entries))

                self._remove_entry(oldest_key)

            now = time.monotonic()

            self._entries[digest] = CacheEntry(
                key_digest=digest,
                package=package,
                size_bytes=package_size,
                created_at=now,
                last_access_at=now,
                expires_at=now + self._ttl_seconds,
            )

            self._total_bytes += package_size

            return True

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # GET OR LOAD — SINGLE-FLIGHT
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def get_or_load(
        self,
        key: str,
        loader: VolumeLoader,
    ) -> VolumePackage:
        """
        Load a missing volume once for concurrent requests.

        One caller becomes the leader. Others wait for it,
        then retrieve the cached result.

        Loaders execute outside the cache lock, avoiding
        a global lock during DICOM decoding.
        """

        digest = normalize_cache_key(key)

        with self._lock:
            self._require_open()

            if self._enabled:
                cached = self._lookup(digest)

                if cached is not None:
                    return cached

            if not self._enabled:
                pending = None
                leader = True
            else:
                pending = self._pending.get(digest)

                leader = pending is None

                if leader:
                    pending = _PendingLoad()
                    self._pending[digest] = pending

            generation = self._generation

        # Cache-disabled mode deliberately skips coordination.
        if not self._enabled:
            result = loader()

            if not isinstance(result, VolumePackage):
                raise CacheLoadFailed()

            return result

        assert pending is not None

        if not leader:
            pending.event.wait()

            if pending.error is not None:
                raise pending.error

            with self._lock:
                self._require_open()

                cached = self._lookup(digest)

                if cached is not None:
                    return cached

            # Cache was invalidated or evicted before
            # the waiting caller resumed.
            raise CacheLoadFailed()

        try:
            result = loader()

            if not isinstance(result, VolumePackage):
                raise CacheLoadFailed()

            with self._lock:
                self._require_open()

                if generation != self._generation:
                    raise CacheLoadFailed()

            self.put(key, result)

            return result

        except CacheServiceError as error:
            pending.error = error
            raise

        except Exception:
            pending.error = CacheLoadFailed()
            raise CacheLoadFailed() from None

        finally:
            with self._lock:
                if self._pending.get(digest) is pending:
                    del self._pending[digest]

                pending.event.set()

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # INVALIDATION
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def invalidate(
        self,
        key: str,
    ) -> bool:
        digest = normalize_cache_key(key)

        with self._lock:
            self._require_open()

            self._generation += 1

            return self._remove_entry(digest)

    def clear(self) -> int:
        """
        Clear cached references.

        Does not delete source or runtime files.
        """

        with self._lock:
            self._require_open()

            count = len(self._entries)

            self._entries.clear()
            self._total_bytes = 0
            self._generation += 1

            return count

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # STATISTICS
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def statistics(self) -> CacheStatistics:
        with self._lock:
            self._remove_expired(time.monotonic())

            return CacheStatistics(
                entries=len(self._entries),
                total_bytes=self._total_bytes,
                total_mib=round(
                    self._total_bytes / MIB, 2
                ),
                hits=self._hits,
                misses=self._misses,
                evictions=self._evictions,
                expirations=self._expirations,
                pending_loads=len(self._pending),
                maximum_entries=self._maximum_entries,
                ttl_seconds=self._ttl_seconds,
                enabled=self._enabled,
                closed=self._closed,
            )

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # CLOSE
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def close(self) -> None:
        """
        Release cache-held references during shutdown.

        In-flight loaders may complete, but cannot insert
        results after the service closes.
        """

        with self._lock:
            if self._closed:
                return

            self._closed = True
            self._generation += 1

            self._entries.clear()
            self._total_bytes = 0


# ============================================================
# 08 — SERVICE FACTORY
# ============================================================

def create_volume_cache(
    settings: AppSettings | None = None,
) -> VolumeCacheService:
    """
    Create a cache instance for the backend application.

    The FastAPI lifespan manager should own the instance
    and call close() during shutdown.
    """

    return VolumeCacheService(settings)


# ============================================================
# 09 — SAFE CACHE SUMMARY
# ============================================================

def get_cache_summary(
    cache: VolumeCacheService,
) -> dict:
    """
    Operational summary without cache keys, DICOM paths,
    patient names, identifiers or imaging pixel data.
    """

    return cache.statistics().safe_summary()


# ============================================================
# END OF CACHE SERVICE
# ============================================================
```

### sinus-viewer/backend/app/services/dataset_service.py
```
"""
SINUS VIEWER — DATASET MANAGEMENT SERVICE
==========================================

Coordinates read-only DICOM dataset discovery and maintains
temporary, process-local patient/series indexes.

Features:
- Authorized-root dataset selection.
- Read-only patient and series discovery.
- Thread-safe dataset session lifecycle.
- Opaque, randomly generated dataset session IDs.
- Caller-owned session isolation.
- Safe patient/series lookup.
- Explicit refresh and cancellation.
- Configurable maximum active sessions.
- Controlled scanner failures.
- No original dataset modification.

SECURITY CONTRACT:
- This service does not authenticate HTTP requests.
- The authenticated API must supply a trusted owner_id
  obtained from its verified server-side session.
- Never use an unverified browser-supplied owner_id.
- Session IDs and patient/series handles are not credentials.
- Never log raw paths, names, UIDs or exception messages.
- Raw index objects must never be serialized directly.

SCANNING CONTRACT:
- scan_dataset() is synchronous and can take substantial time.
- Call it through the backend's bounded background thread pool.
- Indexes are kept in memory, not written into the dataset.
- Source files remain strictly read-only.

Python 3.10+
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import secrets
import threading
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from enum import Enum
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from ..core.config import AppSettings, get_settings
from ..dicom.scanner import (
    DatasetScanResult,
    ScanPatient,
    ScanSeries,
    ScannerCancelled,
    ScannerError,
    ScanStatistics,
    scan_dataset,
    validate_dataset_directory,
)


# ============================================================
# 01 — CONSTANTS
# ============================================================

DEFAULT_MAX_ACTIVE_DATASETS = 1
MAX_OWNER_ID_LENGTH = 256
MAX_HANDLE_LENGTH = 128


# ============================================================
# 02 — SESSION STATE
# ============================================================

class DatasetState(str, Enum):
    READY = "ready"
    SCANNING = "scanning"
    CLOSED = "closed"


# ============================================================
# 03 — CONTROLLED ERRORS
# ============================================================

class DatasetServiceError(RuntimeError):
    """
    Safe service exception.

    Never include patient identities or source paths.
    """

    public_code = "PROCESSING_FAILED"

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        super().__init__(self.public_code)


class DatasetAccessError(DatasetServiceError):
    public_code = "ACCESS_DENIED"


class DatasetSelectionError(DatasetServiceError):
    public_code = "PATH_REJECTED"


class DatasetSessionNotFound(DatasetServiceError):
    public_code = "DATASET_NOT_FOUND"


class DatasetPatientNotFound(DatasetServiceError):
    public_code = "PATIENT_NOT_FOUND"


class DatasetSeriesNotFound(DatasetServiceError):
    public_code = "SERIES_NOT_FOUND"


class DatasetBusyError(DatasetServiceError):
    public_code = "SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE"


class DatasetScanFailed(DatasetServiceError):
    public_code = "PROCESSING_FAILED"


class DatasetOperationCancelled(DatasetServiceError):
    public_code = "OPERATION_CANCELLED"


# ============================================================
# 04 — SESSION MODELS
# ============================================================

@dataclass
class DatasetSession:
    """
    Internal dataset session.

    Sensitive scanner objects are intentionally excluded
    from automatic dataclass representations.
    """

    session_id: str = field(repr=False)
    owner_id: str = field(repr=False)
    dataset_path: Path = field(repr=False)
    result: DatasetScanResult = field(repr=False)

    created_at: float
    updated_at: float

    generation: int = 1
    state: DatasetState = DatasetState.READY

    cancel_event: threading.Event = field(
        default_factory=threading.Event,
        repr=False,
    )

    def safe_summary(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        stats = self.result.statistics

        return {
            "dataset_session_id": self.session_id,
            "state": self.state.value,
            "generation": self.generation,
            "patient_count": (
                stats.patient_count if stats else 0
            ),
            "series_count": (
                stats.series_count if stats else 0
            ),
            "dicom_files": (
                stats.dicom_files if stats else 0
            ),
        }


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DatasetServiceStatistics:
    active_sessions: int
    maximum_sessions: int
    scans_started: int
    scans_completed: int
    scans_failed: int
    scans_cancelled: int
    closed: bool

    def safe_summary(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {
            "active_sessions": self.active_sessions,
            "maximum_sessions": self.maximum_sessions,
            "scans_started": self.scans_started,
            "scans_completed": self.scans_completed,
            "scans_failed": self.scans_failed,
            "scans_cancelled": self.scans_cancelled,
            "closed": self.closed,
        }


# ============================================================
# 05 — TRUSTED OWNER AND HANDLE VALIDATION
# ============================================================

def validate_owner_id(owner_id: str) -> str:
    """
    Validate a trusted server-side identity reference.

    This is not authentication. The caller must have
    authenticated the identity before invoking this service.
    """

    if (
        not isinstance(owner_id, str)
        or not owner_id
        or len(owner_id) > MAX_OWNER_ID_LENGTH
        or any(ord(char) < 33 for char in owner_id)
    ):
        raise DatasetAccessError()

    return owner_id


def validate_handle(handle: str) -> str:
    if (
        not isinstance(handle, str)
        or not handle
        or len(handle) > MAX_HANDLE_LENGTH
        or not all(
            char.isascii()
            and (char.isalnum() or char in "_-")
            for char in handle
        )
    ):
        raise DatasetAccessError()

    return handle


def generate_dataset_session_id() -> str:
    """
    Create an unpredictable lookup identifier.

    API authorization remains mandatory even when a valid
    session ID is supplied.
    """

    return "ds_" + secrets.token_urlsafe(24)


# ============================================================
# 06 — DATASET SERVICE
# ============================================================

class DatasetService:
    """
    Thread-safe controller for read-only dataset indexes.

    Only one scan/refresh is allowed at a time per service.
    Scans are performed outside the main session lock.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        settings: AppSettings | None = None,
        *,
        max_active_datasets: int = DEFAULT_MAX_ACTIVE_DATASETS,
    ) -> None:
        if (
            not isinstance(max_active_datasets, int)
            or isinstance(max_active_datasets, bool)
            or max_active_datasets < 1
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Maximum active datasets must be positive."
            )

        self._settings = (
            settings if settings is not None
            else get_settings()
        )

        self._maximum_sessions = max_active_datasets

        self._sessions: dict[str, DatasetSession] = {}

        self._lock = threading.RLock()
        self._scan_lock = threading.Lock()

        self._closed = False

        self._scans_started = 0
        self._scans_completed = 0
        self._scans_failed = 0
        self._scans_cancelled = 0

        self._active_scan_cancel: (
            threading.Event | None
        ) = None

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # INTERNAL HELPERS
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def _require_open(self) -> None:
        if self._closed:
            raise DatasetBusyError()

    def _get_authorized_session(
        self,
        session_id: str,
        owner_id: str,
    ) -> DatasetSession:
        """
        Caller must hold self._lock when using this helper.

        Return the same non-disclosing error for missing and
        unauthorized session identifiers.
        """

        validate_owner_id(owner_id)
        validate_handle(session_id)

        self._require_open()

        session = self._sessions.get(session_id)

        if (
            session is None
            or session.owner_id != owner_id
            or session.state == DatasetState.CLOSED
        ):
            raise DatasetSessionNotFound()

        return session

    def _scan(
        self,
        dataset_path: Path,
        cancel_event: threading.Event,
    ) -> DatasetScanResult:
        """
        Run scanner under a global single-scan gate.

        Scanning outside the session lock allows other
        sessions to remain readable.
        """

        if not self._scan_lock.acquire(blocking=False):
            raise DatasetBusyError()

        try:
            with self._lock:
                self._require_open()
                self._active_scan_cancel = cancel_event
                self._scans_started += 1

            try:
                result = scan_dataset(
                    dataset_path,
                    self._settings,
                    cancel_event=cancel_event,
                )

                if cancel_event.is_set():
                    raise ScannerCancelled()

                with self._lock:
                    self._scans_completed += 1

                return result

            except ScannerCancelled:
                with self._lock:
                    self._scans_cancelled += 1

                raise DatasetOperationCancelled() from None

            except ScannerError:
                with self._lock:
                    self._scans_failed += 1

                raise DatasetScanFailed() from None

            except Exception:
                with self._lock:
                    self._scans_failed += 1

                raise DatasetScanFailed() from None

        finally:
            with self._lock:
                if self._active_scan_cancel is cancel_event:
                    self._active_scan_cancel = None

            self._scan_lock.release()

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # SELECT DATASET
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def select_dataset(
        self,
        dataset_path: str | Path,
        *,
        owner_id: str,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Scan and register one dataset for a trusted owner.

        Performs no filesystem writes.

        If the service closes while the scan is running,
        the result will not be registered.
        """

        validate_owner_id(owner_id)

        try:
            validated_path = validate_dataset_directory(
                dataset_path,
                self._settings,
            )

        except ScannerError:
            raise DatasetSelectionError() from None

        with self._lock:
            self._require_open()

            if len(self._sessions) >= self._maximum_sessions:
                raise DatasetBusyError()

        cancel_event = threading.Event()

        result = self._scan(
            validated_path,
            cancel_event,
        )

        now = time.monotonic()

        session = DatasetSession(
            session_id=generate_dataset_session_id(),
            owner_id=owner_id,
            dataset_path=validated_path,
            result=result,
            created_at=now,
            updated_at=now,
            cancel_event=cancel_event,
        )

        with self._lock:
            self._require_open()

            # Recheck capacity after scanning because another
            # caller may have registered a session meanwhile.
            if len(self._sessions) >= self._maximum_sessions:
                raise DatasetBusyError()

            self._sessions[session.session_id] = session

            return session.safe_summary()

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # LIST OWNER DATASETS
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def list_datasets(
        self,
        *,
        owner_id: str,
    ) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        validate_owner_id(owner_id)

        with self._lock:
            self._require_open()

            return [
                session.safe_summary()
                for session in self._sessions.values()
                if session.owner_id == owner_id
            ]

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # GET DATASET SUMMARY
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def get_dataset(
        self,
        session_id: str,
        *,
        owner_id: str,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        with self._lock:
            session = self._get_authorized_session(
                session_id,
                owner_id,
            )

            return session.safe_summary()

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # PATIENT DISCOVERY
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def list_patients(
        self,
        session_id: str,
        *,
        owner_id: str,
    ) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        with self._lock:
            session = self._get_authorized_session(
                session_id,
                owner_id,
            )

            return [
                patient.safe_summary()
                for patient in session.result.patients
            ]

    def get_patient(
        self,
        session_id: str,
        patient_handle: str,
        *,
        owner_id: str,
    ) -> ScanPatient:
        """
        Return an internal patient object.

        API routes must serialize only approved fields.
        """

        validate_handle(patient_handle)

        with self._lock:
            session = self._get_authorized_session(
                session_id,
                owner_id,
            )

            patient = session.result.get_patient(
                patient_handle
            )

            if patient is None:
                raise DatasetPatientNotFound()

            return patient

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # SERIES DISCOVERY
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def list_series(
        self,
        session_id: str,
        patient_handle: str,
        *,
        owner_id: str,
    ) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        patient = self.get_patient(
            session_id,
            patient_handle,
            owner_id=owner_id,
        )

        return [
            series.safe_summary()
            for series in patient.series
        ]

    def get_series(
        self,
        session_id: str,
        series_handle: str,
        *,
        owner_id: str,
    ) -> ScanSeries:
        """
        Return an internal series only after owner validation.

        Used by the volume service to locate source metadata.
        """

        validate_handle(series_handle)

        with self._lock:
            session = self._get_authorized_session(
                session_id,
                owner_id,
            )

            series = session.result.get_series(
                series_handle
            )

            if series is None:
                raise DatasetSeriesNotFound()

            return series

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # REFRESH DATASET
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def refresh_dataset(
        self,
        session_id: str,
        *,
        owner_id: str,
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Rescan selected dataset and atomically replace index.

        If scanning fails, the previous successful index
        remains available unless the session was closed.

        A generation change indicates that volume cache
        entries derived from the old index must be invalidated
        by the calling orchestration layer.
        """

        with self._lock:
            session = self._get_authorized_session(
                session_id,
                owner_id,
            )

            if session.state != DatasetState.READY:
                raise DatasetBusyError()

            old_generation = session.generation

            session.state = DatasetState.SCANNING
            session.cancel_event = threading.Event()

            path = session.dataset_path
            cancel_event = session.cancel_event

        try:
            result = self._scan(
                path,
                cancel_event,
            )

            with self._lock:
                session = self._get_authorized_session(
                    session_id,
                    owner_id,
                )

                if (
                    session.generation != old_generation
                    or session.cancel_event is not cancel_event
                ):
                    raise DatasetBusyError()

                session.result = result
                session.generation += 1
                session.updated_at = time.monotonic()
                session.state = DatasetState.READY

                return session.safe_summary()

        finally:
            with self._lock:
                existing = self._sessions.get(session_id)

                if (
                    existing is not None
                    and existing.cancel_event is cancel_event
                    and existing.state == DatasetState.SCANNING
                ):
                    existing.state = DatasetState.READY

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # CANCEL ACTIVE SCAN
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def cancel_active_scan(self) -> bool:
        """
        Internal administrative operation.

        Do not expose as an unauthenticated public API.
        A caller must have administrative authorization.
        """

        with self._lock:
            if self._active_scan_cancel is None:
                return False

            self._active_scan_cancel.set()
            return True

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # CLOSE ONE DATASET
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def close_dataset(
        self,
        session_id: str,
        *,
        owner_id: str,
    ) -> bool:
        with self._lock:
            session = self._get_authorized_session(
                session_id,
                owner_id,
            )

            session.cancel_event.set()
            session.state = DatasetState.CLOSED

            del self._sessions[session_id]

            return True

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # SERVICE STATISTICS
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def statistics(self) -> DatasetServiceStatistics:
        with self._lock:
            return DatasetServiceStatistics(
                active_sessions=len(self._sessions),
                maximum_sessions=self._maximum_sessions,
                scans_started=self._scans_started,
                scans_completed=self._scans_completed,
                scans_failed=self._scans_failed,
                scans_cancelled=self._scans_cancelled,
                closed=self._closed,
            )

    # --------------------------------------------------------
    # SHUTDOWN
    # --------------------------------------------------------

    def close(self) -> None:
        """
        End all dataset sessions and request scan cancellation.

        Caller is responsible for joining the bounded worker
        pool during application shutdown.
        """

        with self._lock:
            if self._closed:
                return

            self._closed = True

            if self._active_scan_cancel is not None:
                self._active_scan_cancel.set()

            for session in self._sessions.values():
                session.cancel_event.set()
                session.state = DatasetState.CLOSED

            self._sessions.clear()


# ============================================================
# 07 — SERVICE FACTORY
# ============================================================

def create_dataset_service(
    settings: AppSettings | None = None,
    *,
    max_active_datasets: int = DEFAULT_MAX_ACTIVE_DATASETS,
) -> DatasetService:
    """
    Create service for FastAPI application lifespan.

    Startup should create one process-local instance,
    and shutdown should call close().
    """

    return DatasetService(
        settings=settings,
        max_active_datasets=max_active_datasets,
    )


# ============================================================
# 08 — SAFE SERVICE SUMMARY
# ============================================================

def get_dataset_service_summary(
    service: DatasetService,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    return service.statistics().safe_summary()


# ============================================================
# END OF DATASET SERVICE
# ============================================================
```

### sinus-viewer/backend/app/services/patient_service.py
